f_{(12)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}\mathbf{0}_{MN}
and

f_{(12)}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})=f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{2}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{2}]}(\mathbf{c}_{2})).

Then

\displaystyle f_{(12)3}(\mathbf{c}_{1}\oplus\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})

\displaystyle=f_{(12)}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})+f_{3}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{3}]}(\mathbf{c}_{3}))

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{2}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{2}]}(\mathbf{c}_{2}))+f_{3}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{3}]}(\mathbf{c}_{3})).

Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle f_{1(23)}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\oplus\mathbf{c}_{3})

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{(23)}(\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{2}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{2}]}(\mathbf{c}_{2}))+f_{3}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{3}]}(\mathbf{c}_{3})).

Then we have

\displaystyle f_{(12)3}*_{\mathbf{0}_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}}\delta_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})}q^{k_{(12)3}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})}\cdot q^{f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{2}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{2}]}(\mathbf{c}_{2}))+f_{3}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{3}]}(\mathbf{c}_{3}))}

and

\displaystyle f_{1(23)}*_{\mathbf{0}_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}}\delta_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})}q^{k_{1(23)}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2},\mathbf{c}_{3})}\cdot q^{f_{1}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{1}]}(\mathbf{c}_{1}))+f_{2}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{2}]}(\mathbf{c}_{2}))+f_{3}(\phi_{[\epsilon_{3}]}(\mathbf{c}_{3}))},

where the second integral in both equations runs over

\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M_{1}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},M_{2}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime\prime},M_{3}}

and the functions
k_{(12)3}
and
k_{1(23)}

compute the dimensions of fibers of the diagonal maps
\phi_{(12)3}
and
\phi_{1(23)}
respectively in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},M_{1}\oplus M_{2}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},M_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M_{1}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime\prime},M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M_{1}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},M_{2}}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime\prime},M_{3}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M_{1},M_{2}\oplus M_{3},\mathbf{0}_{M_{1},M_{2}\oplus M_{3}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2},M_{3},\mathbf{0}_{M_{1}\oplus M_{2},M_{3}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{(12)3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1(23)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\phi_{M_{1},M_{2},\mathbf{0}_{M_{1},M_{2}}}\times{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod{\rm id}\times\phi_{M_{2},M_{3},\mathbf{0}_{M_{2},M_{3}}}}

From the associativity of
\delta_{M}*\delta_{N}
, we know that the diagram commutes. So
k_{(12)3}=k_{1(23)}
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

By definition,
\delta_{M}*\delta_{N}=\delta_{N}*\delta_{M}
. This implies

\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})}.

However, it need not be the case
that
k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})=k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})
, since one value is computed using
\mathbf{0}_{MN}
and the other using

\mathbf{0}_{NM}
.

So even though the weight functions
h
and
h^{\prime}
in

\displaystyle(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})=h*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\delta_{M\oplus N},

\displaystyle(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})*(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})=h^{\prime}*_{\mathbf{0}_{NM}}*\delta_{M\oplus N}

satisfy
h(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})=h^{\prime}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})
by definition, we still can not deduce that

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot q^{h(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})}\cdot q^{h^{\prime}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})}.

3.7
The projectivization of
\boldsymbol{\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}

Since
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
is a finite dimensional vector space, we can consider
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
. We denote the equivalence class of
[\epsilon]
in
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
by
\mathbb{P}[\epsilon]
.

The core aim in this subsection is to check that the multiplication of weighted quantum cluster functions is still well defined if we replace
[\epsilon]
by
\mathbb{P}[\epsilon]
.
In this subsection, we fix a non-zero parameter
\lambda
in the field
k
.
Recall the mapping

\phi_{MN}\colon\ \operatorname{EF}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}}(M,N)\rightarrow\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}

with affine fibers. By Lemma
3.1
and the linearity of
\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
,
[\epsilon]\in p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
if and only if
\lambda[\epsilon]\in p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
. So in this case,

\big|\phi^{-1}_{[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\big|=|K(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])|=|K(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\lambda[\epsilon])|=\big|\phi^{-1}_{\lambda[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\big|.

Otherwise, they are all zero. So we have

Proposition 3.16
.

If
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
satisfy

f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=f^{\prime}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\lambda[\epsilon])\qquad\text{for all}\quad(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N},

then
f*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}=f*_{\lambda[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle f*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}|\phi^{-1}_{[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot q^{f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}|\phi^{-1}_{\lambda[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot q^{f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\lambda[\epsilon])}

\displaystyle=f*_{\lambda[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}}).

∎

Recall that given
f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N}
, we define

(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})=h*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\delta_{M\oplus N},

where
h(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})=f(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime}))+g(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}))
. Since
\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c})=\phi_{\lambda[\epsilon]}(\mathbf{c})
for the corresponding
[\epsilon]
and
\mathbf{c}
, we have

(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})=(f*_{\lambda[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{\mu[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})

for any non-zero
\lambda
and
\mu
in
k
.

3.8
Multiplication of weight functions

Definition 3.17
.

Given weight functions

f,g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
,
f*_{[\eta]}g
is defined by

f*_{[\eta]}g(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=\left\{\begin{aligned} &f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])+g(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon]),&~\text{if}~[\epsilon]=[\eta],\\
&0,&~\text{otherwise}.\end{aligned}\right.

In particular,
f*_{[\eta]}g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}[\eta]
.

Definition 3.18
.

Given
(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})=h*_{\mathbf{0}_{M\oplus N}}\delta_{M\oplus N}
and
[\epsilon]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
, we define
\mathbb{S}_{fg}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}[\epsilon]
by

\mathbb{S}_{fg}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=h(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})=f(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime}))+g(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})).

Corollary 3.19
.

Given
f*_{[\epsilon]^{\prime}}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{[\epsilon]^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{N}
, for any
[\epsilon]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
and
[\eta]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
, we have

\mathbb{S}_{fg}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=\mathbb{S}_{gf}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta]).

Proof.

Both sides are equal to
f(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime}))+g(\phi_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}))
.
∎

3.9
Multiplication formula and balanced pairs

Recall the linear map

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}\colon\ \bigoplus_{j=0}^{m}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(M_{j},N_{j})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{j=0}^{m-1}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(M_{j+1},N_{j}),

\displaystyle([\epsilon_{0}],\dots,[\epsilon_{m}])

\displaystyle\longmapsto([\epsilon_{j-1}]\circ\iota_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},j}-\iota_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},j}\circ[\epsilon_{j}],1\leqslant j\leqslant m)

and its dual

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}=D\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}\colon\ \bigoplus_{j=0}^{m-1}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(N_{j},M_{j+1})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{j=0}^{m}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(N_{j},M_{j})

\displaystyle([\eta_{0}],\dots,[\eta_{m-1}])

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\iota_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},j+1}\circ[\eta_{j}]-[\eta_{j-1}]\circ\iota_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},j},0\leqslant j\leqslant m),

where
[\eta_{-1}]:=\mathbf{0}
and
[\eta_{m}]:=\mathbf{0}
.

So we have

Lemma 3.20
.

For any chains of morphisms
\mathbf{c}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}
ending with
M
,
N
respectively,
\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}=(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}})^{\perp}
. In particular,

\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}+\operatorname{dim}_{k}\bigl(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)\bigr)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N).

Based on this lemma, we introduce several special weight functions.

Definition 3.21
.

There are three weight functions in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
defined as

(1)

f_{\rm hom}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])=k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})-k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})
;

(2)

f^{+}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\bigl(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)\bigr)
;

(3)

f^{-}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])=\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}

for any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbf{c}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{m,M}
,
\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{m,N}
,
[\epsilon]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
and

[\eta]\in\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
.
Note that all of these functions are constant in the extension.

Definition 3.22
.

Given a pair of weight functions
(f^{+},f^{-})
, set

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}\bigr):=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+f^{+}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])},

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}(f^{-}):=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+f^{-}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])}.

This pair is called pointwise balanced if

\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}-1}{q-1}\cdot q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}=\sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}\bigr)+\sigma_{2}(f^{-})

holds for any
M,N\in\mathcal{A}
and
(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
.

Proposition 3.23
.

The following two pairs of weighted functions

(1)

\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext},f_{\rm hom}\bigr)
;

(2)

(0,f^{-}_{\rm ext}+f_{\rm hom})

are pointwise balanced.

Proof.

We first consider

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}(f_{\rm hom})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+f_{\rm hom}((\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}),[\eta])}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}.

Notice that
k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})
and
k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})
are independent of
[\epsilon]
and
[\eta]
as long as they are located in the domain of integration as follows.
Since

\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}+\operatorname{dim}_{k}\bigl(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)\bigr)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N),

we have two equalities

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M))}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}-1}{q-1}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}-1}{q-1}+q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}-1}{q-1}.

So by definition,

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}\bigr)+\sigma_{2}({f_{\rm hom}})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M))}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\bigg(\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}-1}{q-1}\cdot q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M))}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}-1}{q-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}-1}{q-1}

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(0)+\sigma_{2}({f^{-}_{\rm ext}+f_{\rm hom}})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\bigg(\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}-1}{q-1}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}-1}{q-1}\cdot q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}-1}{q-1}.

∎

Theorem 3.24
.

If a pair of weight functions
\bigl(f^{+},f^{-}\bigr)
in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
is pointwise balanced, then for any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N}
,
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Proof.

We simplify the equality in the theorem to
{\rm l.h.s.}=\Sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}\bigr)+\Sigma_{2}(f^{-})
.

Just for simplicity, we omit some independent variables of functions which are obvious in following calculation.
For example,
\mathbb{S}_{fg}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{0}_{MN})
is simplified to

\mathbb{S}_{fg}
.

Direct calculation:

\displaystyle{\rm l.h.s.}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})=\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\phantom{{\rm l.h.s.}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})}{}=\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{fg}}

\displaystyle\phantom{{\rm l.h.s.}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})}{}=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\mathbb{S}_{fg}}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}-1}{q-1}\cdot q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})},

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}\bigr)(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\iiint_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N),\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{fg}+f^{+}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\mathbb{S}_{fg}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+f^{+}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\mathbb{S}_{fg}}\cdot\sigma_{1}\bigl(f^{+}\bigr),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}(f^{-})(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\iiint_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M),\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\in\phi_{[\eta]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{gf}+f^{-}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\mathbb{S}_{fg}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+f^{-}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\mathbb{S}_{fg}}\cdot\sigma_{2}(f^{-}).

By Definition
3.22
, we have
{\rm l.h.s.}=\Sigma_{1}(f^{+})+\Sigma_{2}(f^{-})
.
∎

From Proposition
3.23
and Theorem
3.24
, we have

Theorem 3.25

(multiplication formula of weighted quantum cluster functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
{\rm Hom}
-finite,
\operatorname{Ext}
-finite abelian category with
\operatorname{Ext}
-symmetry over a finite field
k=\mathbb{F}_{q}
such that the iso-classes of objects form a set. For any weighted quantum cluster functions

f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N}
such that
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\mathbb{S}_{fg}*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{[\eta]}f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Theorem
3.24
shows that every pointwise balanced pair is a balanced pair as in the following definition.

Definition 3.26
.

A pair of weight functions
\bigl(f^{+},f^{-}\bigr)
in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
is called a balanced pair if for any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N}
such that
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}

holds.

Thus by definition, any balanced pair satisfies a multiplication formula as in Theorem
3.25
.

3.10
The case
\boldsymbol{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1}

Finally, we simplify the formula in the case that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)=1
, so that
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
and
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
are both singleton sets. Moreover, since
p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
are orthogonal, one of them is of dimension
1
and the other is zero.

Lemma 3.27
.

Assume
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1
, and that
[\epsilon]
and
[\eta]
are non-zero in
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
and
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
, respectively. We have

(1)

If
(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))
, then
f^{+}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])=0
;

(2)

If
(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\in\phi_{[\eta]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))
, then
f^{-}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])=0
.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.2
,
(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))
if and only if
[\epsilon]\in p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}.

In this case,
p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
contains a non-zero element, so
\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}=1
.
By definition,
f^{+}_{\rm ext}((\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}),[\epsilon])=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\bigl(\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)\bigr)=0
.

Dually, by Lemma
3.5
,

(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\in\phi_{[\eta]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))\quad\text{if and only if}\quad[\eta]\in\operatorname{Im}\beta^{\prime}_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M).

In this case,
\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}=0
.
∎

Corollary 3.28
.

Assume that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1
, and that
[\epsilon]
and
[\eta]
are non-zero in
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)
and
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)
, respectively. Then

(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}\bigl(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})\bigr)\qquad\text{if and only if}\quad(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\notin\phi_{[\eta]}\bigl(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime})\bigr).

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1
,
\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}
is either
0
or
1
. Both conditions are equivalent to
\operatorname{dim}_{k}p_{0}\operatorname{Ker}\beta_{\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}}=1
.
∎

Theorem 3.29
.

Assume
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1
and there are nonsplit short exact sequences

\epsilon\colon\ 0\rightarrow N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{ and }\quad\eta\colon\ 0\rightarrow M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\rightarrow 0.

Then we have
(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})=\mathbb{S}_{fg}*{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}+(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}
. In particular,
\delta_{M}*\delta_{N}=\delta_{L}+(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}f)*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}
, where
f
is the zero function in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MF}}
.

Proof.

Recall the multiplication formula

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)}\mathbb{S}_{fg}*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{[\eta]}f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}

from Theorem
3.25
.

Since
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)=1
,
\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(M,N)\big|=1
and both integrals in the formula degenerate into a pair of terms indexed by
[\epsilon]
and
[\eta]
respectively. Then we have

\displaystyle(f*_{[\epsilon^{\prime}]}\delta_{M})*(g*_{[\epsilon^{\prime\prime}]}\delta_{N})={}

\displaystyle\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon]}\delta_{L}+(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{fg}*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}+(f^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{[\eta]}f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}.

From Lemma
3.27
,

\displaystyle\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{[\epsilon]}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{fg}+f^{+}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},[\epsilon])}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\epsilon]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\epsilon],\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{fg}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}_{fg}*_{[\epsilon]}\delta_{L}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

and

\displaystyle(f^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{[\eta]}f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\phi_{[\eta]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{gf}+f^{-}_{\rm ext}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])+f_{\rm hom}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])}

\displaystyle\quad=\iint_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a},(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\in\phi_{[\eta]}(\mathcal{F}^{\rm mono}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}([\eta],\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))}q^{k(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{gf}+f_{\rm hom}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime},[\eta])}

\displaystyle\quad=(f_{\rm hom}*_{[\eta]}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{[\eta]}\delta_{L^{\prime}}(d_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}}).

∎

4
2-Calabi–Yau triangulated categories

and multiplication formula

4.1
2-Calabi–Yau triangulated categories

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\operatorname{Hom}
-finite, 2-Calabi–Yau, Krull–Schmidt triangulated category over a finite field
k=\mathbb{F}_{q}
, which admits a cluster tilting object
T
. The shift functor on
\mathcal{C}
is denoted by
\Sigma
. Let
B
be the endomorphism algebra of
T
, so there is a functor

\displaystyle F:=\operatorname{Hom}(T,-)\colon\ \mathcal{C}\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{mod}B,

\displaystyle X\longmapsto

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}(T,X)

from
\mathcal{C}
to the abelian category
\operatorname{mod}B
. This induces an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{C}/(\Sigma T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{mod}B,

where
(\Sigma T)
denotes the ideal of morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
which factor through a direct sum of copies of
\Sigma T

and
\mathcal{C}/(\Sigma T)
is the corresponding quotient category (see
[
16
]
).
Let
\{S_{1},\dots,S_{n}\}
be a complete set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
\operatorname{mod}B
.

For any two objects
M
,
N
in
\operatorname{mod}B
, define

\displaystyle\langle M,N\rangle=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M,N)-\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{B}^{1}(M,N),\qquad\langle M,N\rangle_{a}=\langle M,N\rangle-\langle N,M\rangle.

The form
\langle-,-\rangle_{a}
can be reduced to the Grothendieck group
K_{0}(\operatorname{mod}B)
(see
[
16
, Lemma 1.3]
).

For any object
X
in
\mathcal{C}
, there are two triangles

T_{2}\rightarrow T_{1}\rightarrow X\rightarrow\Sigma T_{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Sigma T_{4}\rightarrow X\rightarrow\Sigma^{2}T_{3}\rightarrow\Sigma^{2}T_{4}

with
T_{1}
,
T_{2}
,
T_{3}
,
T_{4}
in
{\rm add}T
. So we can define index and coindex of
X

\operatorname{ind}X:=[FT_{1}]-[FT_{2}]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{coind}X:=[FT_{3}]-[FT_{4}],

where
[-]
represents the equivalence class of an object in the Grothendieck group
K_{0}(\operatorname{proj}B)
.

4.2
Exact structure

An element
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
induces a triangle

N\xrightarrow{i}L\xrightarrow{p}M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N

in
\mathcal{C}
. In this case, we denote
{\rm qt}\epsilon=M
,
{\rm st}\epsilon=N
and
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
.
Applying
F
, we get an exact sequence in
\operatorname{mod}B

FN\xrightarrow{Fi}FL\xrightarrow{Fp}FM\xrightarrow{F\epsilon}F\Sigma N.

Assume
M_{0}\subseteq FM
and
N_{0}\subseteq FN
are submodules, and consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}

We denote

{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)=\big\{L_{0}\subseteq FL|Fi^{-1}(L_{0})=N_{0},Fp(L_{0})=M_{0}\big\}.

We now calculate
|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)|
. Recall that
\mathcal{C}
is 2-Calabi–Yau, so for any two objects
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, there are natural isomorphisms

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\rightarrow D\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N),

which induce an isomorphism

D_{M,N}\colon\ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr)\longrightarrow D\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)

and then a bilinear form

\widetilde{D}_{M,N}\colon\ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Sigma^{-1}M,N)\times\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)\longrightarrow k,\,\,(a,b)\longmapsto D_{M,N}(a)(b).

Via the composition of functors
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}/(\Sigma T)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{mod}B
, any two inclusions
M_{0}\subseteq FM
and
N_{0}\subseteq FN

in
\operatorname{mod}B
can be lifted to two morphisms
\tilde{M}_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{M}}M
and
\tilde{N}_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{N}}N
in
\mathcal{C}
, respectively.
Then we can define two linear maps

\displaystyle\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,\tilde{N}_{0}\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}/(T)}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}\tilde{M}_{0},\tilde{N}_{0}\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}\tilde{M}_{0},N\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}/(\Sigma T)}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr),

\displaystyle(a,b)\longmapsto\bigl(a\circ\Sigma^{-1}\iota_{M},\iota_{N}\circ a\circ\Sigma^{-1}\iota_{M}-b\circ\Sigma^{-1}\iota_{M},\iota_{N}\circ a-b\bigr)

and

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\colon

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\Sigma T}\bigl(\tilde{N}_{0},\Sigma\tilde{M}_{0}\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(N,\Sigma\tilde{M}_{0}\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\Sigma^{2}T}(N,\Sigma M)

\displaystyle\quad\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\tilde{N}_{0},\Sigma M\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M),

\displaystyle(a,b,c)\longmapsto(\Sigma\iota_{M}\circ a+c\circ\iota_{N}+\Sigma\iota_{M}\circ b\circ\iota_{N},-c-\Sigma\iota_{M}\circ b),

where
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Sigma T}(-,-)
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Sigma^{2}T}(-,-)
represent the homomorphisms of
\mathcal{C}
which factor through a direct sum of copies of
\Sigma T
and
\Sigma^{2}T
, respectively.

The following properties of
\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}
are given in
[
16
, Lemma 4.2, Proposition 4.3]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, let
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
and choose submodules
M_{0}\subseteq FM
,
N_{0}\subseteq FN
.

(1)

{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
is non-empty if and only if
\Sigma^{-1}\epsilon\in p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
where
p
is the projection from

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,\tilde{N}_{0}\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr)\qquad\text{to}\quad\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\Sigma^{-1}M,N\bigr);

(2)

{\rm Gr}^{\eta}_{N_{0},M_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\eta))
is non-empty if and only if
\eta\in\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
where the intersection is realized through regarding
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
as a linear subspace of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\tilde{N}_{0},\Sigma M\bigr)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M).

Moreover, note that
\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
is the dual of
\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}
with respect to the pairing
\widetilde{D}_{M,N}

[
10
, Lemma 7.3.1]
, so

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
and submodules
M_{0}\subseteq FM
,
N_{0}\subseteq FN
, we have

\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}=(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}})^{\perp}.

In particular,

\operatorname{dim}_{k}p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}+\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N).

Then, to calculate
|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)|
when it is non-zero, we use the following result.

Lemma 4.3
.

If
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
is non-empty, then
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})
acts
freely and transitively on
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon)).

Moreover, if we set

l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0}):=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0}),

then
|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)|=q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}
.

Proof.

We sketch the proof.

Consider the commutative diagram in
\operatorname{mod}B

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN/N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,M_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}}

where
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
and
M_{0}
,
N_{0}
,
L_{0}
are submodules with
Fi^{-1}(L_{0})=N_{0}
,
Fp(L_{0})=M_{0}
.

We can define an action of
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})
on
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
as follows. For any

\displaystyle f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})\qquad\text{and}\qquad L_{0}\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL),

\displaystyle L_{0}^{f}:=\{Fi(n)+x\mid n\in FN,\,x\in L_{0},\,f(Fp(x))=\pi(n)\}

is a linear subspace of
FL
. Since
Fi
,
Fp
,
f
,
\pi
are
B
-module homomorphisms,

B(Fi(n)+x)=B(Fi(n))+B(x)=Fi(B(n))+B(x)

and

f(Fp(B(x)))=B(f(Fp(x)))=B(\pi(n))=\pi(B(n)).

So
L_{0}^{f}
is a submodule of
FL
. For
Fi(n)+x\in L_{0}^{f}
, since
Fp(Fi(n)+x)=Fp(x)\in M_{0}
and
Fi^{-1}(Fi(n))=Fi^{-1}(n_{0})
for some
n_{0}\in N_{0}
,
we have
Fi^{-1}(Fi(n)+x)=Fi^{-1}(n_{0})+Fi^{-1}(x)\in N_{0}
and then
Fi^{-1}\bigl(L^{f}_{0}\bigr)=N_{0}
,
Fp\bigl(L^{f}_{0}\bigr)=M_{0}
. Thus
L^{f}_{0}\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
.

If
f=0
, then
f(Fp(x))=0
and
\pi(n)=0
implies
n\in N_{0}
, so
L^{0}_{0}=L_{0}
. Moreover,
\bigl(L_{0}^{f}\bigr)^{g}=L_{0}^{f+g}
. Since
Fi^{-1}(L_{0})=N_{0}
,
Fi(n)\in L_{0}
if and only if
n\in N_{0}
and in this case,
\pi(n)=0
. So if
L^{f}_{0}=L_{0}
, then
f(Fp(L_{0}))=f(M_{0})=0
. That is to say, the action is free. For any
L^{\prime}_{0}\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
, define
f
as
f(m)=\pi(x^{\prime}-x)
where
x\in L_{0}
and
x^{\prime}\in L^{\prime}_{0}
with
Fp(x)=Fp(x^{\prime})=m
. Then
L^{f}_{0}=L^{\prime}_{0}
, so the action is transitive.
∎

Moreover, according to the proof, we can define a linear structure on
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
with respect to a fixed
L_{0}
in
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
by

\lambda L_{0}^{f}=L_{0}^{\lambda f},\qquad L_{0}^{f}+L_{0}^{g}=L_{0}^{f+g}.

Then the map
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})\rightarrow{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
which sends
f
to
L_{0}^{f}
is a linear isomorphism.

Remark 4.4
.

Notice that
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})
just depends on
M
,
N
,
M_{0}
,
N_{0}
and is independent of
{\rm mt}\epsilon
. So as long as
\Sigma^{-1}\epsilon
is chosen from
p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
, the dimension
l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})
is invariant. That is to say, all
\epsilon
in
\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
lead to the same quantity
|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)|
.

Corollary 4.5
.

All submodules in
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
have the same dimension vector.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3
, any submodule in
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
has the form
L_{0}^{f}
for some
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})
.
Since
L^{f}_{0}\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
, we have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{f}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}}

Both rows are exact at the middle term, therefore
L_{0}^{f}/Fi(N_{0})\cong M_{0}
and then

\operatorname{dim}L_{0}^{f}=\operatorname{dim}Fi(N_{0})+\operatorname{dim}M_{0}.

Summarizing all the analysis in this subsection, we have

Proposition 4.6
.

For any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, let
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and choose submodules
M_{0}\subseteq FM
,
N_{0}\subseteq FN
.

(1)

The set
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
is non-empty if and only if
\epsilon\in\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
if and only if

\epsilon\in\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{M_{0},N_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
;

(2)

If
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))
is non-empty, then there is a bijection

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})\longrightarrow{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))

and

|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))|=q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}
.

4.3
Quantum cluster functions

Let
A_{n,\lambda}
be the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra generated by
x_{1}^{\pm},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm}
with defining relations: for any
{\bf e}=(e_{1},\dots,\allowbreak e_{n})
,
{\bf f}=(f_{1},\dots,f_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
,

X^{\bf e}\cdot X^{\bf f}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda({\bf e},{\bf f})}X^{{\bf e}+{\bf f}},

where
X^{\bf e}=x_{1}^{e_{1}}\dots x_{n}^{e_{n}}
and
X^{\bf f}=x_{1}^{f_{1}}\dots x_{n}^{f_{n}}
are monomials in
A_{n,\lambda}
and
\lambda(-,-)
is a skew-symmetric bilinear form. Such
A_{n,\lambda}
is called a skew-polynomial algebra and elements in
A_{n,\lambda}
are called skew-polynomials.

On the other hand, for any
L\in\mathcal{C}
and
{\bf d}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
, the set of all submodules
F_{0}
of
FL
with dimension vector
{\bf d}
is denoted by
{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)
, called a quiver Grassmannian.

Definition 4.7
.

For each object
L
in
\mathcal{C}
, we assign a skew-polynomial in
A_{n,\lambda}
as

X_{L}:=\int_{{\bf d}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)|\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})},

where the integral runs over all
{\bf d}
in
\mathbb{N}^{n}
and
p(L,{\bf d})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
with

p(L,{\bf d})_{i}=-({\rm coind}L)_{i}+\langle\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}S_{i},{\bf d}\rangle_{a}.

The skew-polynomial is called a quantum cluster function of
L
.

Notice that the set of all
{\bf d}
such that
{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)
is non-empty is finite, so the integral is just a finite sum.

4.4
Mappings with affine fibers

Given
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
, define

\operatorname{EG}(M,N):=\biggl\{(\epsilon,L_{0})\mid\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N),\,L_{0}\in\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(F({\rm mt}\epsilon))\biggr\}.

Then from
(\epsilon,L_{0})\in\operatorname{EG}(M,N)
, we can induce two submodules
M_{0}
,
N_{0}
of
FM
and
FN
respectively as
N_{0}=i^{-1}(L_{0})
and
M_{0}=p(L_{0})
. Let
L:={\rm mt}\epsilon
.
Then we naturally have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}

We denote this assignment by

\psi_{MN}\colon\ \operatorname{EG}(M,N)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN),

where the coproduct runs over
\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{n}
.

Fix
\epsilon
, and set

\psi_{\epsilon}:=\psi_{MN}(\epsilon,-)\colon\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN).

To separate all pieces indexed by
{\bf d}
, we set

\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}:=\psi_{\epsilon}|_{{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)}\colon{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN).

Moreover, denote

{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f}):=\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}({\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN))

and

{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d}):=\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}({\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f})).

Notice that
{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
may not be the whole of
{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
because
\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}
is not surjective in general.

Rewriting Proposition
4.6
in this notation, we get the following.

Lemma 4.8
.

Given
(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
and
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
,
If
(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
, then
\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})
acts
freely and transitively on
\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})
.

4.5
Cardinality

In this subsection, we calculate cardinalities of sets involved in several lemmas above and refine the calculation of
X_{L}
.

Considering
\psi_{MN}
, since
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f})=\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}({\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN))
and the Grassmannian is a finite set here, we have

|{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)|=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f})|

for any
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
, and

|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f})|=\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|.

Moreover, based on Lemma
4.8
, we have the following.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
and
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
.

(1)

If
(M_{0},N_{0})\notin{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
,
\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|=0
;

(2)

If
(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
,
\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|=|\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(M_{0},FN/N_{0})|=q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}
.

Given these calculations, if
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
, we can refine the formula for
X_{L}
to

\displaystyle X_{L}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)|\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{\bf d}(FL,{\bf e},{\bf f})|\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

Remark 4.10
.

Similar to the case in Section
3
, the refinement of
X_{L}
still depends on the choice of
\epsilon
which is not unique. But, although different
\epsilon
lead to different refinements, they all evaluate
to
X_{L}
in the end.

4.6
Weight

Based on the refinements of
X_{L}
, we introduce weight functions and weighted quantum cluster functions.

Define

\operatorname{MG}:=\biggl\{(M_{0},N_{0})\mid(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN),\,M,N\in\mathcal{C}\biggr\}

and set

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}:=\big\{f\colon\operatorname{MG}\times\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\rightarrow Z\mid f(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=0\text{ unless }M={\rm qt}\epsilon,\,N={\rm st}\epsilon\big\},

where
\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}=\coprod_{M,N\in\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
Z=\big\{\frac{n}{2}\mid n\in\mathbb{Z}\big\}
is the set of all half integers.
The functions in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
are called weight functions.
Given
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(M,\Sigma N)
, we define

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon):=\{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}\mid f(M_{0},N_{0},\rho)=0\text{ if }\rho\neq\epsilon\}.

Given
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
, we write
f(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)
instead as
f(M_{0},N_{0})
.

Definition 4.11

(weighted quantum cluster function)
.

Given a weight function
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}({\epsilon})
, the weighted quantum cluster function
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
is a skew-polynomial in
A_{n,\lambda}
defined as

\displaystyle f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot q^{f(M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot q^{f(M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})},

where
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
with
M={\rm qt}\epsilon
,
N={\rm st}\epsilon
and
L={\rm mt}\epsilon
.

Similarly, we can take
f
to be the zero function to check that this definition is a
q
-deformation of the quantum cluster function.

For the zero function
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
,
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=X_{L}
.

4.7
Multiplication

We denote the zero morphism in
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
by
\mathbf{0}_{MN}
. First, we consider the multiplication of quantum cluster functions.
Recall that the multiplication of skew-polynomials follows the generating relation
X^{\bf e}\cdot X^{\bf f}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda({\bf e},{\bf f})}X^{{\bf e}+{\bf f}}
.

So given
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, we can multiply the quantum cluster functions as

\displaystyle X_{M}\cdot X_{N}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)|\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot\int_{{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)|\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)|\cdot|{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)|\cdot q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}.

On the other hand, we calculate
X_{M\oplus N}
as

\displaystyle X_{M\oplus N}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN},{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,{\bf d})}.

Comparing these two equalities, there are three differences: the power of
X
, the domain of integration, and the power of
q
. We analyze them successively.

Firstly, given
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
and submodules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}

where
N_{0}=Fi^{-1}(L_{0})
,
M_{0}=Fp(L_{0})
, we denote the dimension vectors
of
M_{0}
,
N_{0}
and
L_{0}
by
{\bf e}
,
{\bf f}
and
{\bf d}
respectively.

Lemma 4.12

(
[
16
, Lemma 5.1]
)
.

With the notation above,
p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})=p(L,{\bf d})
.

This lemma allows us to identify the powers of
X
in
X_{M}\cdot X_{N}
and
X_{M\oplus N}
, providing we are careful about the indices
{\bf e}
,
{\bf f}
and
{\bf d}
of the terms in these sums.

Secondly, recall the map

\psi_{\epsilon}=\psi_{MN}(\epsilon,-)\colon\ \coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)

and pieces

\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}=\psi_{\epsilon}|_{{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)}\colon\ {\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN).

Although
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{M_{0},N_{0}}(FL)
can contain many submodules, they all have
the same dimension vector by Corollary
4.5
. That is, the sets
{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
do not intersect for different values of
{\bf d}
.
Thus the data
({\bf e},{\bf f},M_{0},N_{0})
indexing a term in the expression for
X_{M}\cdot X_{N}
uniquely determines the additional datum
{\bf d}
needed to index a term in the expression for
X_{M\oplus N}
. Moreover, for
\mathbf{0}_{MN}
,

\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\colon\ \coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}(FL)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)

is surjective because any two submodules of
FM
and
FN
can be assembled into a submodule of
F(M\oplus N)
through direct sum. So in this case, for any
{\bf e}
,
{\bf f}
,

{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)=\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}(FM,FN,{\bf d}).

Then we have

\displaystyle X_{M}\cdot X_{N}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

Finally, we introduce a special family of weight functions.

Definition 4.13
.

For any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, define a weight function
{f}_{\rm spec}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
by

{f}_{\rm spec}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})

if
M_{0}\in\coprod_{\bf e}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)
,
N_{0}\in\coprod_{\bf f}{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
,
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
, and
0
otherwise.

Note that
{f}_{\rm spec}
is constant in
\epsilon
. The following is then immediate.

Proposition 4.14
.

For any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
, in
A_{n,\lambda}

X_{M}\cdot X_{N}={f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N}.

Definition 4.15
.

Given weight functions

f,g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
,
f*_{\eta}g
is defined by

\displaystyle f*_{\eta}g(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\begin{cases}f(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)+g(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon),&\text{if}\ \epsilon=\eta,\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

for
M_{0}\in\coprod_{{\bf e}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)
and
N_{0}\in\coprod_{{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
. That is to say,
f*_{\eta}g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\eta).

Now we consider the multiplication
(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})
. Obviously, the product must contain information about the weight functions
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon^{\prime})
and
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon^{\prime\prime})
. To record these, we define a corresponding weight function for the middle term.

Definition 4.16
.

Given weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
, and a morphism
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
, define a weight function
\mathbb{T}_{fg}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
by

\mathbb{T}_{fg}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=f(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0}))+g(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0})).

By Definition
4.16
, given weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
, for any
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
, we have

\mathbb{T}_{fg}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\mathbb{T}_{gf}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)

since both sides are equal to
f(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0}))+g(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0})).

Proposition 4.17
.

For any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
, in
A_{n,\lambda}
we have

(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})=({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N}.

Proof.

For simplicity, without causing ambiguity, we omit some variables of weight functions in following calculation. For example,

\mathbb{T}_{fg}(M_{0},N_{0},\mathbf{0}_{MN})
is simplified to

\mathbb{T}_{fg}
.

The key step is to calculate the fibers of the following composition of mappings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf e}_{1}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}_{1}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf e}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf e}_{2}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf d}}(F(M\oplus N))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf f}_{1}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}_{1}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{{\bf f}_{1}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}_{1}}(F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}\circ\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}\circ\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}\circ\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}\circ\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}\circ\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}\circ\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}}

Notice that
\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}
is surjective, but
\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}
and
\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
may not be surjective. We have

\displaystyle\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})={\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN),

\displaystyle\coprod_{{\bf e}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),{\bf e})\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}_{1}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}))\times{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle\coprod_{{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon_{N}),F({\rm st}\epsilon_{N}),{\bf f})\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}_{1}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}))\times{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}_{2}}(F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime})).

By Definitions
4.11
and
4.13
, and Proposition
4.14
, we obtain

\displaystyle({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})+\mathbb{T}_{fg}+{f}_{\rm spec}(M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN)}q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e}}\int_{M_{0}\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)}X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\int_{{\bf f}}\int_{N_{0}\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN)}X^{p(M,{\bf f})}\cdot q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}.

Recall from Lemma
4.9

{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)=\coprod_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\bf e}}(FM,{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2})=\coprod_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}\psi^{-1}_{\epsilon^{\prime},{\bf e}}\bigl({\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime}),{\bf e})\bigr)

with the dimension of the fiber being
l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime},M_{1},M_{2})
and

{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN)=\coprod_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{{\bf f}}(FN,{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2})=\coprod_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}\psi^{-1}_{{\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},\epsilon^{\prime\prime},{\bf f}}\bigl({\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),{\bf f})\bigr)

with the dimension of the fiber being
l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},N_{1},N_{2})
.

Then we have

\displaystyle({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e}}\int_{M_{0}\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e}}(FM)}X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\int_{{\bf f}}\int_{N_{0}\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f}}(FN)}X^{p(M,{\bf f})}\cdot q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e}}\int_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}\int_{(M_{1},M_{2})\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime}),{\bf e})}q^{l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime},M_{1},M_{2})}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}\cdot\!\int_{{\bf f}}\int_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}\int_{(N_{1},N_{2})\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),{\bf f})}q^{l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},N_{1},N_{2})}\!\cdot\!X^{p(N,{\bf f})}\!\cdot\!q^{f(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0}))+g(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0}))}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e}}\int_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}\int_{(M_{1},M_{2})\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\bf e}_{1},{\bf e}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime}),{\bf e})}q^{l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime},M_{1},M_{2})}\cdot q^{f(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0}))}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}\cdot\int_{{\bf f}}\int_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}\int_{(N_{1},N_{2})\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}_{{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}}(F({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),F({\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime}),{\bf f})}q^{l({\rm qt}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},{\rm st}\epsilon^{\prime\prime},N_{1},N_{2})}\cdot q^{g(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0}))}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle\quad=(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}).

∎

4.8
The projectivization of
\boldsymbol{\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}

Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
is a finite dimensional vector space, we can consider
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
. We denote the equivalence class of
\epsilon
in
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
by
\mathbb{P}\epsilon
.

In this subsection, we check that multiplication of weighted quantum cluster functions is still well defined if we replace
\epsilon
by
\mathbb{P}\epsilon
.
We assume the parameter
\lambda
is a non-zero element in
k
.

Recall the mapping

\psi_{MN}\colon\ \operatorname{EG}(M,N)\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)

with affine fibers.

By Lemma
4.8
and the linearity of
\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
,
\epsilon\!\in\!\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
if and only if
\lambda\epsilon\!\in\!\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
. So in this case,

\big|\psi^{-1}_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|=q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}=\big|\psi^{-1}_{\lambda\epsilon,{\bf d}}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|.

Otherwise, they are both zero. So we have the following.

Proposition 4.18
.

Given
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
, set
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\lambda\epsilon)
with

f(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=f(M_{0},N_{0},\lambda\epsilon)
.
Then
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=f*_{\lambda\epsilon}X_{L}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\epsilon,{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot q^{f(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\lambda\epsilon_{L},{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot q^{f(M_{0},N_{0},\lambda\epsilon)}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=f*_{\lambda\epsilon}X_{L}.

∎

Note that given
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
, by Proposition
4.17
, we have

(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})=({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N},

where
{f}_{\rm spec}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
and

\mathbb{T}_{fg}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=f(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0}))+g(\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0})).

If we replace
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
by their scalar multiple on the left-hand side, since
\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0})=\psi_{\lambda\epsilon^{\prime}}(M_{0})
and
\psi_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0})=\psi_{\mu\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(N_{0})
for any non-zero
\lambda
and
\mu
in
k
,
we have

(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})=(f*_{\lambda\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\mu\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}).

If we replace
\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
by their scalar multiple on the right-hand side, since
{f}_{\rm spec}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
is constant in any
\epsilon
and
\lambda\mu\mathbf{0}_{MN}=\mathbf{0}_{MN}
,
the right-hand side also remains the same.

4.9
Multiplication formula and balanced pairs

Firstly, we introduce several special weight functions and an important property.

Definition 4.19
.

There are three weight functions in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
defined as

(1)

g_{\rm skew}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)=\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))
, where
\lambda(-,-)
is the skew-symmetric bilinear form defined in Section
4.3
;

(2)

g^{+}_{\rm ext}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))
;

(3)

g^{-}_{\rm ext}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}

for any
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
,
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
,
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
.

Note that the three weight functions are all independent
of the extension
\eta
or
\epsilon
.

Definition 4.20
.

Given a pair of weight functions
(g^{+},g^{-})
, set

\sigma_{1}(g^{+}):=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}q^{g^{+}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)}

and

\sigma_{2}(g^{-}):=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}q^{g^{-}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)}.

This pair is called pointwise balanced if

\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}=\sigma_{1}(g^{+})+\sigma_{2}(g^{-})

holds for any
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
and
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
.

Proposition 4.21
.

The following two pairs of weight functions

(1)

\bigl(g^{+}_{\rm ext},0\bigr)
;

(2)

(0,g^{-}_{\rm ext})

are pointwise balanced.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
4.2
that for any
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
and
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
,
we have

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}+\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N).

After projectivization, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}-1}{q-1}+q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}-1}{q-1}.

So by definition,

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(g^{+}_{\rm ext})+\sigma_{2}(0)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}q^{g^{+}_{\rm ext}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}1

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}1

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}\!-1}{q-1}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}\!-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(0)+\sigma_{2}(g^{-}_{\rm ext})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}1+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}q^{g^{-}_{\rm ext}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}1+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}-1}{q-1}+q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}.

∎

Theorem 4.22
.

If a pair of weight functions
\bigl(g^{+},g^{-}\bigr)
in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
is pointwise balanced, then for any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
such that
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Proof.

We simplify the equality in the theorem to
{\rm l.h.s.}=\Sigma_{1}(g^{+})+\Sigma_{2}(g^{-})
. Just for simplicity, we omit some independent variables without causing ambiguity in following calculation.
For example,
\mathbb{T}_{fg}((M_{0},N_{0}),\epsilon)
is simplified as
\mathbb{T}_{fg}
,
l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})
as
l
and
{f}_{\rm spec}((M_{0},N_{0}),\epsilon)
as
{f}_{\rm spec}
.

Direct calculation:

\displaystyle{\rm l.h.s.}

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}X_{M\oplus N}

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l+\mathbb{T}_{fg}+{f}_{\rm spec}}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,{\bf d})}
