\displaystyle=|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}.

Recall that the third equality in the above is based on the fact

\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})={\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN).

For general
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
, we only have

\displaystyle\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN),

\displaystyle\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}^{\eta}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}(FN,FM,{\bf d})\subseteq{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM),

and whether a pair
(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
belongs to
{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
or
{\rm Gr}^{\eta}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}(FN,\allowbreak FM,{\bf d})
is determined by Proposition
4.6
.

So we can calculate the right-hand side as

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}(g^{+})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l+g^{+}+\mathbb{T}_{fg}+{f}_{\rm spec}}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf d}}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{g^{+}+\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}q^{g^{+}+\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\mathbb{T}_{fg}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}\cdot\sigma_{1}(g^{+}).

Notice that in the following calculation of
\Sigma_{2}(g^{-})
, we denote
l=l(N,M,N_{0},M_{0})
,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}(g^{-})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}q^{l+g^{-}+\mathbb{T}_{gf}+{f}_{\rm spec}+g_{\rm skew}}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in\coprod_{g}{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}q^{g^{-}+\mathbb{T}_{gf}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)}\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}q^{g^{-}+\mathbb{T}_{gf}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)}q^{\mathbb{T}_{gf}}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}\cdot\sigma_{2}(g^{-}).

Then by Definitions
4.16
and
4.20
, we have
{\rm l.h.s.}=\Sigma_{1}(g^{+})+\Sigma_{2}(g^{-})
.
∎

From Proposition
4.21
and Theorem
4.22
, we have

Theorem 4.23

(multiplication formula of weighted quantum cluster functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
{\rm Hom}
-finite,
2
-Calabi–Yau, Krull–Schmidt triangulated category over a finite field
k=\mathbb{F}_{q}
with a cluster tilting object
T
. For any weighted quantum cluster functions

f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
such that
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}(g^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Moreover, we can define a balanced pair as

Definition 4.24
.

A pair of weight functions
\bigl(g^{+},g^{-}\bigr)
in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}
is called a balanced pair if for any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
such that

{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}

holds.

4.10
The case
\boldsymbol{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1}

In this subsection, we assume

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)=1

and the triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\xrightarrow{\eta}\Sigma M

are non-split. Thus
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
and
\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
are both singleton sets represented by
\mathbb{P}\epsilon
and
\mathbb{P}\eta
respectively. Since
\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
and
\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
are orthogonal, one of them is of dimension
1
and the other is zero.

Lemma 4.25
.

With assumptions above:

(1)

If
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}
,
g^{+}_{\rm ext}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=0
;

(2)

If
(N_{0},M_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\eta}
,
g^{-}_{\rm ext}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)=0
.

Proof.

If
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}
, then
\epsilon\in\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
. Since
\epsilon
is non-zero,
\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
is of dimension
1
. Thus

g^{+}_{\rm ext}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))=0.

On the other hand, if
(N_{0},M_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\eta}
, then
\eta\in\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)
. Since
\eta
is non-zero, we have
g^{-}_{\rm ext}(N_{0},M_{0},\eta)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}=0
.
∎

Corollary 4.26
.

With assumptions above,

(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}\qquad\text{if and only if}\quad(N_{0},M_{0})\notin\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\eta}.

Proof.

Both statements are equivalent to
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}=1
. ∎

By Lemma
4.2
, we have

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}+\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N),

and

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}-1}{q-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}-1}{q-1}+q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}}\cdot\frac{q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))}-1}{q-1}.

So in the case
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
, one of
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\Sigma p\operatorname{Ker}\alpha_{M_{0},N_{0}}
and

\operatorname{dim}_{k}(\operatorname{Im}\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}\cap\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M))

is
1
and the other is
0
.
Then
g^{+}_{\rm ext}
and
g^{-}_{\rm ext}
are zero in the relevant domain of integration and the two balanced pairs given in Proposition
4.21
both degenerate to
(0,g_{\rm skew})
.

Theorem 4.27
.

Assume
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
and the triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\xrightarrow{\eta}\Sigma M

are non-split triangles. Then we have

(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})=({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}X_{L}+(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{L^{\prime}}.

5
Connection with preprojective algebras

In this section, we provide a quantum analogue of the connections in

[
12
]
between Palu’s multiplication formula for cluster characters
[
17
]
and Geiss–Leclerc–Schröer’s multiplication formula for evaluation forms
[
10
]
.

5.1
Preprojective algebra and nilpotent modules

Let
k
be a finite field and
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1},s,t)
be a finite quiver where
Q_{0}=\{1,\dots,n\}
is the vertex set, and for an arrow
\alpha\colon i\rightarrow j
in
Q_{1}
, set
s(\alpha)=i
and
t(\alpha)=j
. We can obtain a new quiver
\widetilde{Q}
from
Q
by adding a new arrow
\bar{\alpha}\colon j\rightarrow i
for each arrow
\alpha\colon i\rightarrow j
in
Q_{1}
. Define

c:=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in Q_{1}}\alpha\bar{\alpha}-\bar{\alpha}\alpha,

and let
\Lambda:=k\widetilde{Q}/\langle c\rangle
be the preprojective algebra of
Q
. For each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
S_{i}
be the simple
\Lambda
-module associated to the vertex
i
.
Denote the category of all nilpotent
\Lambda
-modules by
\operatorname{nil}\Lambda
. Let
\hat{I_{i}}
be the injective envelope of
S_{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Given an element
\omega
in the Weyl group associated to
Q
,
Buan, Iyama, Reiten, and Scott
[
2
]
have attached to
\omega
a 2-Calabi–Yau Frobenius
subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}\subset\operatorname{nil}\Lambda
. One can refer to
[
11
, Section 2.4]
for a detailed description of
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
. We fix the element
\omega
and a reduced expression
\mathbf{i}
.

In
\operatorname{nil}\Lambda
, there is a classical definition of a flag.

Definition 5.1
.

A flag
L_{\bullet}
of
L
in
\operatorname{nil}\Lambda
is a series of submodules

0=L_{m}\subseteq L_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq L_{0}=L.

Moreover, a flag
L_{\bullet}
is called of type
(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a})
if
L_{j-1}/L_{j}
is isomorphic to
S_{i_{j}}^{\oplus a_{j}}
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
.
Denote the set of all flags of
L
of type
(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a})
by
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
.

Note that any flag of type
(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a})
can be refined to a flag of type
(\mathbf{i}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime})
with
\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.
As in Section
2
, set

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}:=\big\{L_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{L,m}}}{{\longrightarrow}}L_{m-1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots{\longrightarrow}L_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{L,1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}L_{0}=L\mid L_{j}\in\operatorname{nil}\Lambda,\iota_{L,j}\text{ is mono},1\leqslant j\leqslant m\big\}.

Consider the action of the group
\prod_{i=0}^{m}\operatorname{Aut}L_{i}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
as follows.
For any

\tilde{g}=(g_{0},g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in\displaystyle\prod_{i=0}^{m}\operatorname{Aut}L_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\iota_{L,m},\iota_{L,m-1},\dots,\iota_{L,1}),

define

\tilde{g}.(\iota_{L,m},\iota_{L,m-1},\dots,\iota_{L,1}):=\bigl(g^{-1}_{m-1}\iota_{L,m}g_{m},g^{-1}_{m-2}\iota_{L,m-1}g_{m-1},\dots,g^{-1}_{0}\iota_{L,1}g_{1}\bigr),

which can be illustrated by the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}\,\,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{m-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{L,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{L,m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{L,1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{-1}_{m-1}\iota_{L,m}g_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{-1}_{m-2}\iota_{L,m-1}g_{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{m-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{-1}_{0}\iota_{L,1}g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{0}}

Note that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
is the set of orbits of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
under the action of
\prod_{i=0}^{m}\operatorname{Aut}L_{i}
and

\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
is the set of orbits of
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
under the action of the group

\biggl\{(g_{0},g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in\displaystyle\prod_{i=0}^{m}\operatorname{Aut}L_{i}|g_{0}=\operatorname{id}_{L}\biggr\}.

Hence
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
can be viewed as the set of orbits of
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
under the action of the group

\biggl\{(g_{0},\operatorname{id}_{L_{1}},\dots,\operatorname{id}_{L_{m}})\in\displaystyle\prod_{i=0}^{m}\operatorname{Aut}L_{i}\biggr\}\simeq\operatorname{Aut}L.

Note that for
\mathbf{c}_{L}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
,
the cardinality of the stabilizer of
\mathbf{c}_{L}
is
q^{t}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}
(for example, see
[
20
, Section 4.1]
).

The above characterizes
the relationship between
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
when
\mathbf{a}\in\{0,1\}^{m}
. In the following, we will consider
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}

instead of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}^{\rm mono}
as Geiss–Leclerc–Schröer did in
[
12
]
.

For a short exact sequence
\epsilon\colon 0\longrightarrow N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\longrightarrow}}L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}M\longrightarrow 0
, define a map

\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}\colon\ \Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\longrightarrow\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}

which maps a flag

\displaystyle f_{L}:=(0=L_{m}\subseteq L_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq L_{0}=L)\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\quad\text{to}

\displaystyle(f_{M}:=(0=M_{m}\subseteq M_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq M_{1}\subseteq M_{0}=M),

\displaystyle f_{N}:=(0=N_{m}\subseteq N_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq N_{1}\subseteq N_{0}=N)\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}

with
M_{i}=p(L_{i})
and
N_{i}=i^{-1}(L_{i})
for
0\leqslant i\leqslant m
. For any

(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
, if
\bar{\phi}^{-1}_{\epsilon}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\neq\varnothing
, then

\big|\bar{\phi}^{-1}_{\epsilon}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\big|=\big|\bar{\phi}^{-1}_{{\bf 0}_{MN}}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\big|
and
\bar{\phi}^{-1}_{{\bf 0}_{MN}}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})
is a vector space (see
[
10
, Lemma 3.3.1]
).
We set

\bar{k}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}):=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\bar{\phi}^{-1}_{{\bf 0}_{MN}}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}).

5.2
Refined socle and top series

In this subsection, we recall some notations and definitions from
[
12
, Section 3.4]
.

Let
L
be an
\Lambda
-module and
S
be a simple
\Lambda
-module. Let
{\rm soc}_{S}L
be the sum of all submodules of
L
which are isomorphic to
S
. If there exists no such
U
, set
{\rm soc}_{S}L=0
.
Similarly, let
{\rm top}_{S}L:=L/V
where
V
is the intersection of all submodules
U
of
L
such that
L/U
are isomorphic to
S
. If there exists no such submodule, then
V=L
and
{\rm top}_{S}L=0
.
Define
{\rm rad}_{S}L:=V
.

For
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})
, there exists a unique chain of submodules

0=L_{m}\subseteq L_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq L_{0}\subseteq L

such that
L_{j-1}/L_{j}={\rm soc}_{S_{i_{j}}}L/L_{j}
. We define
{\rm soc}_{\mathbf{i}}L:=L_{0}
and
L_{j}^{+}:=L_{j}^{+,\mathbf{i}}:=L_{j}
.

Moreover, the chain is denoted by

L_{\bullet}^{+}:=(L_{m}^{+}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{0}^{+}).

In particular, if
{\rm soc}_{\mathbf{i}}L=L
,
L_{\bullet}^{+}
is called the refined socle series of type
\mathbf{i}
of
L
.

Similarly, there exists a unique chain of submodules

0\subseteq L_{m}\subseteq L_{m-1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq L_{0}=L

such that
L_{j-1}/L_{j}={\rm top}_{S_{i_{j}}}L_{j-1}
. We define
{\rm top}_{\mathbf{i}}L:=L/L_{m}
,
{\rm rad}_{\mathbf{i}}L:=L_{m}
and
L_{j}^{-}:=L_{j}^{-,\mathbf{i}}:=L_{j}
.

Moreover, the chain is denoted by

L_{\bullet}^{-}:=(L_{m}^{-}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{0}^{-}).

In particular, if
{\rm rad}_{\mathbf{i}}L=0
,
L_{\bullet}^{-}
is called the refined top series of type
\mathbf{i}
of
L
.

Lemma 5.2

(
[
12
, Lemma 3.5]
)
.

For any
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
, we have
{\rm soc}_{\mathbf{i}}L=L
and
{\rm rad}_{\mathbf{i}}L=0
.

Lemma 5.3

(
[
12
, Lemma 3.8]
)
.

For any
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
and any flag

L_{\bullet}=(0=L_{m}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{0}=L)\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L},

we have
L_{j}^{-}\subseteq L_{j}\subseteq L_{j}^{+}
for
0\leqslant j\leqslant m
.

The above lemma implies that the refined socle series and the refined top series are the maximal flag and the minimal flag, respectively.

5.3
Construction of cluster tilting objects

Assume for each
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, there exists a
j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, such that
i=i_{j}
.
We define

\displaystyle j^{-}:={\rm max}\{0,1\leqslant s\leqslant j-1\mid i_{s}=i_{j}\},\qquad j^{+}:={\rm min}\{j+1\leqslant s\leqslant m,m+1\mid i_{s}=i_{j}\},

\displaystyle j_{{\rm max}}:={\rm max}\{1\leqslant s\leqslant r\mid i_{s}=i_{j}\},\qquad j_{{\rm min}}:={\rm min}\{1\leqslant s\leqslant r\mid i_{s}=i_{j}\},

\displaystyle j_{i}:={\rm max}\{1\leqslant s\leqslant m\mid i_{s}=i\}.

We define
V_{0}:=0
and
V_{j}:={\rm soc}_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{j})}\hat{I}_{i_{j}}
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
. Moreover, let
V_{\mathbf{i}}:=\bigoplus^{m}_{l=1}V_{l}
. For
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
I_{\mathbf{i},i}:=V_{j_{i}}
and
I_{\mathbf{i}}:=\bigoplus^{n}_{l=1}I_{\mathbf{i},l}
.

Remark 5.4

(
[
11
, Theorems 2.9 and 2.10]
)
.

The module
V_{\mathbf{i}}
is a
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
-cluster-tilting object.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
is a Frobenius category, we can consider its stable category
[
11
, Theorem 2.8]
. Let
\mathcal{I}(M,N)
be the subspace of
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda}(M,N)
consisting of all morphisms factoring through objects in
{\rm add}I_{\mathbf{i}}
and define

\overline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\Lambda}(M,N)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda}(M,N)/\mathcal{I}(M,N).

Then we have

Proposition 5.5

(
[
12
, Proposition 3.24]
)
.

For any
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
and
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
,

D\overline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\Lambda}(L,V_{j})\cong e_{i_{j}}\bigl(L^{+}_{j}/L^{-}_{j}\bigr),

where
e_{i}
is the primitive idempotent in
\Lambda
associated to the vertex
i
.

5.4
Quiver Grassmannians

Define
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}:=\operatorname{End}_{\Lambda}(V_{\mathbf{i}})^{{\rm op}}
and
\underline{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathbf{i}}:=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}(V_{\mathbf{i}})^{\rm op}
. If
k
is algebraically closed, then
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}
is a finite-dimensional basic algebra and the corresponding quiver can be constructed explicitly. In this section, we make the assumption that over our
fixed finite field
k
, the algebra
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}
is presented by the same quiver with relations as in the case that
k
is algebraically closed.

Define the quiver
Q_{\mathbf{i}}
as follows: the vertex set is
\{1,\dots,m\}
; for each pair of subscripts
1\leqslant k
,
j\leqslant m
satisfying
k^{+}\geqslant j^{+}\geqslant k>j
and each arrow
\alpha\colon i_{j}\rightarrow i_{k}
, there is an arrow
\gamma^{k,j}_{\alpha}\colon j\rightarrow k
called the ordinary arrow; for each
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
, there is an arrow
\gamma_{j}\colon j\rightarrow j^{-}
if
j^{-}\neq 0
called the horizontal arrow.

Proposition 5.6

(
[
12
, Proposition 3.25]
)
.

There is an isomorphism of quivers
Q_{\mathbf{i}}\rightarrow Q_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}
which maps
j
to
V_{j}
.

Moreover, for
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
, the
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}
-module
D\overline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}(L,V_{\mathbf{i}})
can be realized as follows:
the vector space at the vertex
j
is
D\overline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}(L,V_{j})=e_{i_{j}}\bigl(L_{j}^{+}/L_{j}^{-}\bigr)
; for the ordinary arrow
\gamma^{k,j}_{\alpha}\colon j\rightarrow k
, the linear map is given by

e_{i_{j}}\bigl(L_{j}^{+}/L_{j}^{-}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot}e_{i_{k}}\bigl(L_{k}^{+}/L_{k}^{-}\bigr)
; for the horizontal arrow
\gamma_{j}\colon j\rightarrow j^{-}
, the linear map is given by

e_{i_{j}}\bigl(L_{j}^{+}/L_{j}^{-}\bigr)\xrightarrow{e_{i_{j}}\cdot}e_{i_{j}}\bigl(L_{j^{-}}^{+}/L_{j^{-}}^{-}\bigr)
.

5.5
Bijection

Given
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
, define the map

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{i},L}\colon\ \{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\mid\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\neq\varnothing\}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\big\{g\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\mid{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{g}(FL)\neq\varnothing\big\},

\displaystyle(a_{1},\dots,a_{m})

\displaystyle\longmapsto(g_{1},\dots g_{m}),

where
g_{j}=(a_{j}^{-}-a_{j})+(a^{-}_{j^{-}}-a_{j_{-}})+\dots+(a^{-}_{j_{{\rm min}}}-a_{j_{{\rm min}}})
and the map

{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\colon\ \Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\longrightarrow{\rm Gr}^{\underline{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a})}(FL),

where
FL=D\overline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}(L,V_{\mathbf{i}})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(W_{\mathbf{i}},L)
.
For a given
L_{\bullet}=(0=L_{m}\subseteq\dots\subseteq L_{0}=L)\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
, the image
{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}(L_{\bullet})
is a submodule of
FL
which can be realized as follows: the vector space at the vertex
j
is
e_{i_{j}}(L_{j}/L^{-}_{j})
; for the ordinary arrow
\gamma^{k,j}_{\alpha}\colon j\rightarrow k
, the linear map is given by

e_{i_{j}}(L_{j}/L_{j}^{-})\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot}e_{i_{k}}(L_{k}/L_{k}^{-})
;
for the horizontal arrow
\gamma_{j}\colon j\rightarrow j^{-}
, the linear map is given by

e_{i_{j}}(L_{j}/L_{j}^{-})\xrightarrow{e_{i_{j}}\cdot}e_{i_{j}}(L_{j^{-}}/L_{j^{-}}^{-})
.

Theorem 5.7

(
[
12
, Theorem 3.27]
)
.

For any
L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
and
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
such that
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\neq\varnothing
,
the maps
d_{\mathbf{i},L}
and
{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
are bijective.

For the proof of this theorem, one can refer to
[
12
, Theorem 1]
where Geiss, Leclerc and Schröer proved an isomorphism of algebraic varieties from
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
to a certain quiver Grassmannian over the complex field
\mathbb{C}
. This result degenerates to a bijection between finite sets on a finite field
k
, therefore here we state the result without providing a detailed proof.

The above theorem provides the relation between flags and Grassmannians. Furthermore, in order to discover relations between multiplication formulas, we need the following commutative diagram.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
M,N,L\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
where
L={\rm mt}\epsilon
for some
[\epsilon]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(M,N)
. The short exact sequence
\epsilon
provides a triangle
N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N
after stabilization which we still denote by
\epsilon
. Then there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Gr}^{\underline{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a})}(FL)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})}(FN)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}\,\,}

Proof.

Consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a})}(FL)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}^{\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{i}}}_{d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})}(FN)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}\,\,}

Denote
{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
,
{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}
and
{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
by
\bar{F}_{L}
,
\bar{F}_{M}
and
\bar{F}_{N}
respectively. Given

L_{\bullet}\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
, we claim that

\bigl(\bar{F}_{M}\times\bar{F}_{N}\bigr)\bigl(\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(L_{\bullet})\bigr)=\psi_{\epsilon}\bigl(\bar{F}_{L}\bigr).

For
\epsilon\colon N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\longrightarrow}}M{\longrightarrow}\Sigma N
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(L_{\bullet})=\bigl(q(L_{\bullet}),p^{-1}(L_{\bullet})\bigr),\qquad F(\epsilon)\colon\ FN\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Fp}}{{\longrightarrow}}FL\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Fq}}{{\longrightarrow}}FM\longrightarrow,

\displaystyle e_{i_{k}}(F(\epsilon))\colon\ e_{i_{k}}\bigl(N_{k}^{+}/N_{k}^{-}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}e_{i_{k}}\bigl(L_{k}^{+}/L_{k}^{-}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\longrightarrow}}e_{i_{k}}\bigl(M_{k}^{+}/M_{k}^{-}\bigr){\longrightarrow}.

Denoted by
\bar{p}
the induced map of
p
and
\bar{p}_{k}:=e_{i_{k}}\bar{p}
. Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(N_{k}^{+}/N_{k}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(L_{k}^{+}/L_{k}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(M_{k}^{+}/M_{k}^{-})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(p^{-1}(L_{k})/N_{k}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(L_{k}/L_{k}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{i_{k}}(q(L_{k})/M_{k}^{-})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{p}_{k}\,\,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{q}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{p}|_{p^{-1}({L_{k}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{q}|_{L_{k}}}

To prove
\bigl(\bar{F}_{M}\times\bar{F}_{N}\bigr)\bigl(\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(L_{\bullet})\bigr)=\psi_{\epsilon}\bigl(\bar{F}_{L}\bigr)
, it suffices to show that

(a)

\bar{q}|_{L_{k}}
is surjective which follows from the surjectivity of
q|_{L_{k}}\colon L_{k}\longrightarrow q(L_{k})
;

(b)

since
p\colon N\!\longrightarrow L
is injective,
\operatorname{Ker}\bar{p}_{k}=p^{-1}(L_{k}^{-})\cap N_{k}^{+}/N_{k}^{-}=p^{-1}(L_{k}^{-})/N_{k}^{-}=\operatorname{Ker}(\bar{p}|_{p^{-1}({L_{k}})})
;

(c)

\operatorname{Ker}(\bar{q}|_{L_{k}})=\operatorname{Im}(\bar{p}|_{p^{-1}(L_{k})})
which follows by chasing the above diagram.

Therefore, the above diagram is commutative.
We can obtain the commutative diagram described in the theorem.
∎

By Theorem
5.8
, we have

Corollary 5.9
.

For any
(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
, under the same assumptions as in Theorem
5.8
, the map

{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}\colon\ \bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\psi_{\epsilon}^{-1}({\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}(M_{\bullet}),{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}(N_{\bullet}))

is a bijection. In particular,

\big|\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\big|=\big|\psi_{\epsilon}^{-1}({\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}(M_{\bullet}),{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}(N_{\bullet}))\big|.

In order to keep compatibility with Sections
2
and
3
, throughout the rest of this section, we only handle
\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L}
with
\mathbf{a}\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.

5.6
The skew-polynomial corresponding to
\boldsymbol{\Delta_{L}}

In this subsection, we introduce a variant of the quantum cluster function defined in Section
3
.

Notice that
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
is a 2-Calabi–Yau Frobenius subcategory of
\operatorname{nil}\Lambda
with
\operatorname{Ext}
-symmetry,
so that after stabilization,
\underline{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}
is a 2-Calabi–Yau triangulated category.

We define

\operatorname{FL}:=\biggl\{(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\mid(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\,\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\,\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N},\,M,N\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega},\,\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{m}\biggr\}

and set

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}:=\{f\colon\operatorname{FL}\times\operatorname{Exact}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}\rightarrow Z\mid f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\epsilon)=0\text{ unless }\mathbf{c}^{\prime}_{0}={\rm qt}\epsilon,\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime}_{0}={\rm st}\epsilon\},

where
\operatorname{Exact}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}=\{\epsilon\colon 0\rightarrow N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0\mid\epsilon\text{ is a short exact sequence, }N,L,M\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}\}
. The functions in
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}
are called weight functions. Given
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Exact}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}
, we define

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[\epsilon]:=\{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}\mid f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\rho)=0\text{ if }\rho\neq\epsilon\}.

For
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[\epsilon]
, we write
f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime},\epsilon)
instead as
f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})
.

Definition 5.10
.

Given a weight function
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[\epsilon]
,
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
is the skew-polynomial in
A_{m,\lambda}
defined by

f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}:=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\int_{(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L})}q^{\bar{k}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot q^{f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot X^{p(L,d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a}))},

where
p(L,d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a}))
is defined as in Definition
4.7
with
L
being treated as an object in
\underline{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}
.

For the convenience of calculation in the following, we use the notation

f*_{\epsilon}\delta_{L}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\colon=\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\int_{(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})\in\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L})}q^{\bar{k}(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot q^{f(\mathbf{c}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime\prime})}\cdot X^{p(L,d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a}))}.

Notice that
A_{m,\lambda}
is defined in Section
4.3
and we take the same skew polynomial algebra for
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
as the one where
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
is
located. The skew-polynomial
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
is also called the weighted quantum cluster function of
L
here.

To state the corresponding multiplication formula of
f*_{\epsilon_{L}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
, we need two specific weight functions.

Definition 5.11
.

For any objects
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
, define the weight function
{f}_{\rm spec}
by

{f}_{\rm spec}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet},\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))-\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})

if
M_{\bullet}\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}
,
N_{\bullet}\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
, and
0
otherwise.

Definition 5.12
.

For any objects
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
, define the weight function
f_{\rm skew}
as

f_{\rm skew}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\epsilon)=\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))

if
M_{\bullet}\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}
,
N_{\bullet}\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}
, and
0
otherwise.

Then we have

Proposition 5.13
.

For any objects
M,N\in\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
and any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N}
, in
A_{m,\lambda}

we have

(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N})=({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}.

Proof.

For simplicity, without causing ambiguity, we omit some variables of weight functions in following calculation. For example,
{f}_{\rm spec}((M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet}),\epsilon)
is simplified as
{f}_{\rm spec}
.
Direct calculation shows

\displaystyle(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\delta_{M}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}))\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{N}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}))\cdot X^{p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))}\cdot X^{p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\delta_{M}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}))\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{N}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}))\cdot q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot X^{p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime}))+p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\delta_{M}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}))\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{N}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}))\cdot q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,d_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}(\mathbf{a}))}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\delta_{M\oplus N}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,d_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}(\mathbf{a}))}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\int_{(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})}\cdot q^{f+g+{f}_{\rm spec}}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,d_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}(\mathbf{a}))}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\int_{(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\in\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}}q^{f+g}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,d_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}(\mathbf{a}))}.

Then by definitions of
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\delta_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\delta_{N}
, we can obtain the equality.
∎

Theorem 5.14
.

For any weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N}
such that
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}(M,N)\neq 0
, in
A_{m,\lambda}
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)}(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)}({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f^{-}_{\rm ext}*_{\eta}f_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta},

where
f^{+}_{\rm ext}
,
f^{-}_{\rm ext}
and
f_{\rm skew}
are defined as in Definition
4.19
.

Proof.

We only prove the first equality. The calculation for the second one is similar.

Recall that

\displaystyle{f}_{\rm spec}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet},\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})))-\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet}),

\displaystyle{f}_{\rm spec}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime})),p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})))-\bar{k}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet}),

\displaystyle f_{\rm skew}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)

\displaystyle=\lambda(p(M,d_{\mathbf{i},M}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime})),p(N,d_{\mathbf{i},N}(\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}))),

\displaystyle f_{\rm hom}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)

\displaystyle=\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})-\bar{k}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet}).

So we have

f_{\rm skew}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)+{f}_{\rm spec}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)=f_{\rm hom}(N_{\bullet},M_{\bullet},\eta)+{f}_{\rm spec}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet},\epsilon).

Then we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)\big|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},N})

\displaystyle\quad=\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)\big|({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\big|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)\big|({f}_{\rm spec}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\mathbb{S}_{fg})*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\delta_{M\oplus N}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,d_{\mathbf{i},M\oplus N}(\mathbf{a}))},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(M,N)}\bigl(f^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{S}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}\delta_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p({\rm mt}\epsilon,d_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\epsilon}(\mathbf{a}))},

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p({\rm mt}\eta,d_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta}(\mathbf{a}))}.

We can rewrite

\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p({\rm mt}\eta,d_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta}(\mathbf{a}))}

as

\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}^{1}(N,M)}(f_{\rm hom}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{S}_{gf})*_{\eta}\delta_{{\rm mt}\eta}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p({\rm mt}\eta,d_{\mathbf{i},{\rm mt}\eta}(\mathbf{a}))}.

But in the calculation of
q
-powers, we can replace
q^{(f_{\rm skew}*(\mathbb{S}_{gf},\eta)*{f}_{\rm spec})}
by
q^{(f_{\rm hom}*(\mathbb{S}_{gf},\eta)*{f}_{\rm spec})}.
Then the desired equality is a direct consequence of Theorem
4.23
.
∎

Definition 5.15
.

We denote the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra generated by all weighted quantum cluster functions
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
, where
L
runs over
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
and
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[{\epsilon}]
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
, by
A_{q}^{p}(\mathcal{C}_{\omega})
.

Definition 5.16
.

We denote the
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra generated by
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
, where
L
runs over
\underline{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}}
and
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
, by
A_{q}(\underline{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}})
.

5.7
Connection between two multiplication formulas

Now we have introduced multiplication rules and proved multiplication formulas for both
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}
and
g*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
. To end this section, we give the relationship between weight functions.

Given a weight function
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[\epsilon]
, we define a weight function
\mathrm{FG}(f)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
by

\mathrm{FG}(f)(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=f\bigl({\mathrm{FG}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}(M_{0}),{\mathrm{FG}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}(N_{0})\bigr)

for any

(M_{0},N_{0})\in\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN),

where

\mathbf{a}^{\prime}=d_{{\mathbf{i}},M}^{-1}({\bf e})
and
\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=d_{{\mathbf{i}},N}^{-1}({\bf f})
.

Similarly, given a weight function
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
, we define a weight function

\mathrm{FG}^{*}(g)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{FL}}[\epsilon]
by

\mathrm{FG}^{*}(g)(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet},\epsilon)=g({\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}(M_{\bullet}),{\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}(N_{\bullet}),\epsilon)

for any
(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\in\coprod_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}\times\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}.

Moreover, we can extend the definitions of
\mathrm{FG}
and
\mathrm{FG}^{*}
to
A_{q}^{p}(\mathcal{C}_{\omega})
and

A_{q}(\underline{\mathcal{C}_{\omega}})
.

Definition 5.17
.

Define
\mathrm{FG}
as a map from
A_{q}^{p}(\mathcal{C}_{\omega})
to
A_{q}(\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{\omega})
by

\mathrm{FG}(f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L})=\mathrm{FG}(f)*_{\epsilon}X_{L}

and
\mathrm{FG}^{*}
as a map from
A_{q}(\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{\omega})
to
A_{q}^{p}(\mathcal{C}_{\omega})
by

\mathrm{FG}^{*}(g*_{\epsilon}X_{L})=\mathrm{FG}^{*}(g)*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}.

Theorem 5.18
.

The map
\mathrm{FG}
is an isomorphism of algebras with the inverse
\mathrm{FG}^{*}
.

Proof.

In fact, if we consider
A_{q}^{p}(\mathcal{C}_{\omega})
and
A_{q}(\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{\omega})
as subalgebras of a fixed
A_{m,\lambda}
,
\mathrm{FG}
and
\mathrm{FG}^{*}
are identities. Set
\mathrm{FG}(f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L})=g*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
.

By Definition
5.10
,

\displaystyle f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{a}}f*_{\epsilon}\delta_{L}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}})\cdot X^{p(L,d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a}))}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{a}}\int_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbf{a}}\int_{(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})\in\bar{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\Phi_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a},L})}q^{\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})}\cdot q^{f(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})}\cdot X^{p(L,d_{\mathbf{i},L}(\mathbf{a}))}

and

g*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=\int_{\bf d}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot q^{g(M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

By Corollary
5.9
,
\bar{k}(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})=l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})
and by Definition
5.17
,

q^{f(M_{\bullet},N_{\bullet})}=q^{\mathrm{FG}(f)(M_{0},N_{0})}

for

M_{0}={\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime},M}(M_{\bullet})
and
N_{0}={\mathrm{FG}}_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime},N}(N_{\bullet})
.
Then we have
f*_{\epsilon}\Delta_{\mathbf{i},L}=g*_{\epsilon}X_{L}.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

By Definition
5.10
, when
L
is a
\mathcal{C}_{\omega}
-projective-injective object,
FG(L)\in\mathbb{Q}
.

6
Special version in hereditary case

In this section, we consider the cluster category from a hereditary algebra and the corresponding multiplication formula.

6.1
Cluster category from a hereditary algebra

Let
m\geqslant n
be two positive integers and
\widetilde{Q}
an acyclic quiver with the vertex set
\{1,\dots,m\}
. Let
Q
be the full subquiver of
\widetilde{Q}

with the vertex set
\{1,\dots,n\}
. Given a finite field
k
, set
\widetilde{A}=k\widetilde{Q}
and
A=kQ
. For any vertex
i
of
\widetilde{Q}
(respectively
Q
), denote by

S_{i}
the simple
\widetilde{A}
-module (respectively
A
-module) at
i
and by
P_{i}
the indecomposable projective
\widetilde{A}
-module corresponding to
i
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
be the category of finite-dimensional left
\widetilde{{A}}
-modules and the cluster category of

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
introduced by Buan–Marsh–Reineke–Reiten–Todorov
[
3
]
, is defined as
\mathcal{C}\colon=\mathcal{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}=D^{b}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\bigr)/\tau^{-1}\Sigma
where
D^{b}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\bigr)
is the bounded derived category of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
,
\tau
is the Auslander–Reiten translation and
\Sigma
is the shift functor. Respectively, one can define
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}=D^{b}(\mathcal{A})/\tau^{-1}\Sigma
.

Let
\widetilde{B}=(b_{ij})
be an
m\times n
-matrix where

b_{ij}=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}(S_{i},S_{j})-\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}(S_{j},S_{i})

for
1\leqslant i\leqslant m,1\leqslant j\leqslant n.

Assume there exists a skew-symmetric
m\times m
-matrix
\Lambda
such that

\Lambda(-\widetilde{B})={\begin{bmatrix}I_{n}\\
0\end{bmatrix}}_{m\times n},

where
I_{n}
is the
n\times n
identity matrix.

In the following, the bilinear form
\lambda
is always given by

\lambda({\bf e},{\bf f})={\bf e}^{T}\Lambda{\bf f}
.

The cluster category
\mathcal{C}
is a 2-Calabi–Yau triangulated category
[
14
]
with a cluster tilting object
T=\Sigma\widetilde{A}
.
There is a natural functor
F\colon=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(\widetilde{A},-):\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
which induces an
equivalence of categories
\mathcal{C}/\operatorname{add}\Sigma\widetilde{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
.

Moreover, all iso-classes of indecomposable objects in
\mathcal{C}
can be classified by

\operatorname{ind}\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{ind}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\cup\{\Sigma P_{1},\dots,\Sigma P_{m}\},

where
\operatorname{ind}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
is the iso-classes of all indecomposable objects in
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
. We say
L\in\mathcal{C}
is located in the fundamental domain if
L\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
.
An object
M\in\mathcal{C}
is called coefficient-free if
M
does not contain a direct summand
P_{i}[1]
,
i>n
.

In particular, if
M,N\in\operatorname{ind}\mathcal{A}
and
\operatorname{dim}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
, there exist two non-split triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\rightarrow\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\rightarrow\Sigma M,

where one of
L
and
L^{\prime}
is located in the fundamental domain and the other is not
[
13
]
. Without loss of generality, in the following, we always assume
L^{\prime}
is located in the fundamental domain.

Now we can introduce weighted quantum cluster functions and corresponding multiplication formulas given in Section
4
to the cluster category
\mathcal{C}
.

6.2
A special weighted quantum cluster function

Let
L
be an object with
\boldsymbol{\ell}:=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}FL
in
\mathcal{C}
and consider the trivial triangle

L\xrightarrow{\,=\,}L\rightarrow 0\xrightarrow{\sigma_{L}}\Sigma L.

Definition 6.1
.

Given
L
as above, define a special weight function
{f}_{L}
as

{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L}):=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}L_{0},\boldsymbol{\ell}-\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}L_{0}\rangle

for any
L_{0}\in\coprod_{\bf d}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}FL
and
0
otherwise, where
\langle-,-\rangle
is the Euler form of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
.

Definition 6.2
.

With respect to
{f}_{L}
, define the weighted quantum cluster function

\tilde{X}_{L}:={f}_{L}*_{\sigma_{L}}X_{L}.

Notice that, for a given dimension vector
{\bf d}
of a submodule of
FL
, the appropriate dimension vectors of
{\rm qt}\sigma_{L}
and
{\rm st}\sigma_{L}
are unique and obviously
{\mathbf{0}}
and
{\bf d}
. So we can simplify the calculation of
\tilde{X}_{L}
to

\tilde{X}_{L}=\int_{\bf d}\int_{(\mathbf{0},{\bf d})}\int_{(0,L_{0})}q^{{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),{\sigma_{L}})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}=\int_{\bf d}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}FL|\cdot q^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf d},{\bf l}-{\bf d}\rangle}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

Note that Rupel
[
21
]
firstly gave the above definition of quantum cluster characters over cluster categories of hereditary algebras over finite fields.
Qin provided an alternative definition of quantum cluster characters via Serre polynomials
[
18
]
.

Remark 6.3
.

Note that
f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=(f*_{\epsilon}{f}_{L})*_{\epsilon}X_{L}
, with support area still decided by
\epsilon
instead of
\sigma_{L}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
, we have

\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}=\tilde{{f}}_{MN}*_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}\tilde{X}_{M\oplus N}

in
A_{n,\lambda}
where
\tilde{{f}}_{MN}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\mathbf{0}_{MN})
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{MN}(M_{0},N_{0},\mathbf{0}_{MN})={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})

\displaystyle+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})-{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})

for any
M_{0}\in\coprod_{\bf e}{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)
,
N_{0}\in\coprod_{\bf f}{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

We can calculate both sides as

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf e}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)|\cdot q^{{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),{\sigma_{M}})}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})}\cdot\int_{{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)|\cdot q^{{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),{\sigma_{N}})}\cdot X^{p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)||{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)|

\displaystyle\cdot q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),{\sigma_{M}})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),{\sigma_{N}})}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),{\sigma_{M}})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),{\sigma_{N}})}

\displaystyle\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{M\oplus N}={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}(FM)\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}(FN)}\big|\psi_{\mathbf{0}_{MN},{\bf d}}^{-1}(M_{0},N_{0})\big|\cdot q^{{f}_{0}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}(FM,FN,g)}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}

\displaystyle\cdot X^{p(M\oplus N,{\bf d})}.

Then by definition of weighted quantum cluster functions, one can easily check such defined
\tilde{{f}}_{MN}
is the appropriate weight function to satisfy the lemma.
∎

Moreover, if we set
\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}FM={\bf m}
,
\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}FN={\bf n}
, then

Lemma 6.5

(
[
18
]
)
.

With the notation above, we have

\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))=\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e},{\bf n}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e},{\bf f}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf f},{\bf e}\rangle.

By this lemma, we can simplify the calculation of
\tilde{{f}}_{MN}
to

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{MN}(M_{0},N_{0},\mathbf{0}_{MN})={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})+{{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})}

\displaystyle+{{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})}-{{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e},{\bf n}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e},{\bf f}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf f},{\bf e}\rangle

\displaystyle-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf f},{\bf n}-f\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e}+{\bf f},{\bf m}+{\bf n}-{\bf e}-{\bf f}\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0}).

6.3
Special version of multiplication formula

Recall that given any balanced pair of weight functions
\bigl(g^{+},g^{-}\bigr)
and weighted quantum cluster functions

f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M}
and
g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N}
such that
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|(f*_{\epsilon^{\prime}}X_{M})\cdot(g*_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}X_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{fg}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{gf})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

In particular, we can take
\tilde{X}_{M}={f}_{M}*_{\sigma_{M}}X_{M}
and
\tilde{X}_{N}={f}_{N}*_{\sigma_{N}}X_{N}
, then we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{{f}_{\rm spec}}*_{\epsilon}{\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{M}{f}_{N}}}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}X_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{{f}_{\rm spec}}*_{\eta}{\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{N}{f}_{M}}})*_{\eta}X_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

To express the right-hand side in terms of
\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}
and
\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\eta}
, we need to extend
\tilde{{f}}_{MN}
to the non-split case.

Definition 6.6
.

Given
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
and
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L
, define
\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{MG}}(\epsilon)
as

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})

\displaystyle+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})-{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})

for
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}
where
L_{0}
satisfies
\psi_{\epsilon}(L_{0})=(M_{0},N_{0})
, and
0
otherwise.

Remark 6.7
.

Note that

(1)

although the submodule
L_{0}
which satisfies
\psi_{\epsilon}(L_{0})=(M_{0},N_{0})
is not unique, according to Corollary
4.5
, the dimension vector of
L_{0}
is independent of choice, so
\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}
is well defined;

(2)

by definition,
\tilde{{f}}_{\mathbf{0}_{MN}}=\tilde{{f}}_{MN}
;

(3)

if the triangle induced by
\epsilon
is mapped by
F
to a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow FN\rightarrow FL\rightarrow FM\rightarrow 0,

then for any
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}
,

\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)=\tilde{{f}}_{MN}(M_{0},N_{0},\mathbf{0}_{MN}).

Lemma 6.8
.

For any balanced pair
\bigl(g^{+},g^{-}\bigr)
and morphisms
\epsilon\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)
,
\eta\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\allowbreak\Sigma M)
with
{\rm mt}\epsilon=L,{\rm mt}\eta=L^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{M}{f}_{N}}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L},

\displaystyle(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\eta}\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{N}{f}_{M}})*_{\eta}X_{L^{\prime}}=\bigl(g^{+}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}.

Proof.

We only prove the first equality. The proof of the second equality is similar. By definition, the right-hand side is equal to
\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{L}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}{X}_{L}
. We just need to compare the values that
both weight functions take at any
((M_{0},N_{0}),\epsilon)
satisfying
(M_{0},N_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}
. We have

\displaystyle g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{M}{f}_{N}}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)={}

\displaystyle g^{+}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0}).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle g^{+}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{L}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)={}

\displaystyle g^{+}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)+\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})

\displaystyle+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})

\displaystyle-{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})+{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle g^{+}(M_{0},N_{0},\epsilon)+\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))-l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})

\displaystyle+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N}).

Recall the definition of weighted quantum cluster function

f*_{\epsilon}X_{L}=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{l(M,N,M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot q^{f(M_{0},N_{0})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

Since
\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\epsilon}=\coprod_{\bf d}\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
, the weight functions

g^{+}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{\rm spec}*_{\epsilon}\mathbb{T}_{{f}_{M}{f}_{N}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad g^{+}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}{f}_{L}

have the same values on the whole domain of integration. Hence the weighted quantum cluster functions are the same.
∎

Then we can express the right-hand side of the multiplication formula in terms of
\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}
and
\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\eta}
.

Theorem 6.9
.

Given a balanced pair of weight functions
\bigl(g^{+},g^{-}\bigr)
and two objects
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
such that
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}\bigl(g^{-}*_{\eta}g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Recall that Proposition
4.21
provides two pointwise balanced pairs of weight functions.

Corollary 6.10
.

Given two objects
M
,
N
in
\mathcal{C}
such that
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)|\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\bigl(g^{+}_{\rm ext}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}\bigr)*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta})*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathbb{P}\epsilon\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)}\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\epsilon}+\int_{\mathbb{P}\eta\in\mathbb{P}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(N,\Sigma M)}\bigl(g^{-}_{\rm ext}*g_{\rm skew}*\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{{\rm mt}\eta}.

Remark 6.11
.

As this work was being completed, we became aware of a
similar result by Chen, Ding and Zhang
[
7
]
for hereditary categories. The exact
relationship between these two results will be investigated in the near future.

In particular, if
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
, both balanced pairs degenerate to
(0,g_{\rm skew})
. Thus we have

Theorem 6.12
.

Given two objects
M,N\in\mathcal{C}
with
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
and two non-split triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\xrightarrow{\eta}\Sigma M,

we have

\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}=\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}+\bigl(g_{\rm skew}*\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}.

6.4
Recalculation and simplification

Theorem
6.12
is a special case of Theorem
4.27
which was proved generally in Section
4.10
. But in this subsection, we calculate the right-hand side again to obtain a simple expression.
We always assume
M
,
N
are indecomposable coefficient-free rigid objects in
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
with
{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1}
and
the triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\xrightarrow{\eta}\Sigma M

are non-split, where
L^{\prime}
is located in the fundamental domain.

By definition of weighted quantum cluster functions and Lemma
6.5
,

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}\!q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\big(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\big)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})}\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}\!q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{N\oplus M}((0,N_{0}\oplus M_{0}),\sigma_{N\oplus M})}\!\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}.

Remark 6.13
.

One can check the left-hand side in Theorem
6.12
is

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}FM\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}FN}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(p(M,{\bf e}),p(N,{\bf f}))+{f}_{M}((0,M_{0}),\sigma_{M})+{f}_{N}((0,N_{0}),\sigma_{N})}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}FM\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}FN}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot X^{p(M,{\bf e})+p(N,{\bf f})}.

Notice that in the case when
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1
,
{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
is complementary to
{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})
. That is to say,

{\rm Gr}_{\bf e}FM\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf f}FN=\coprod_{\bf d}({\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})\cup{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})).

Thus the above calculation also provides a direct proof of Theorem
6.12
.

Now we focus on

\displaystyle\bigl(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}\!\!q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{N\oplus M}((0,N_{0}\oplus M_{0}),\sigma_{N\oplus M})}\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}.

Recall the assumption that
L^{\prime}
is located in the fundamental domain, so

0\rightarrow FM\rightarrow FL^{\prime}\rightarrow FN\rightarrow 0

is a short exact sequence.

The following result is implicitly implied by an argument used by Qin in
[
18
, Proposition 5.4.1]
.

Lemma 6.14
.

With the assumptions above, if
\psi_{\eta}(L^{\prime}_{0})=(N_{0},M_{0})
as in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/M_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN/N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\colon}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{0}\colon}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{N}}

then
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM/M_{0})=0
.

Proof.

Notice that
\widetilde{A}
is hereditary, thus
\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}(-,-)=0
. Applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}(N_{0},-)
on

0\rightarrow M_{0}\rightarrow FM\rightarrow FM/M_{0}\rightarrow 0,

we get

\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},M_{0})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},i_{M})}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM/M_{0})\rightarrow 0.

Similarly, applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}(-,FM)
on

0\rightarrow N_{0}\rightarrow FN\rightarrow FN/N_{0}\rightarrow 0,

we get

\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(FN/N_{0},FM)\rightarrow\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(FN,FM)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(i_{N},FM)}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM)\rightarrow 0.

Since
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(FN,FM)=1
, we have that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM/M_{0})
is at most one. If
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM/M_{0})=1
, then
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},FM)=1
,
\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},i_{M})
is zero and
\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(i_{N},\allowbreak FM)
is a linear isomorphism between one dimensional vector spaces. However, considering the construction of
\alpha^{\prime}_{N_{0},M_{0}}
, such a commutative diagram implies that
\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(N_{0},i_{M})(\epsilon_{0})
and
\operatorname{Ext}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}^{1}(i_{N},FM)(\epsilon)
coincide when
\epsilon
is non-zero, as we are assuming. This is a contradiction.
∎

Recalling Remark
6.7
, for a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow FM\rightarrow FL^{\prime}\rightarrow FN\rightarrow 0

and
(N_{0},M_{0})\in\operatorname{Im}\psi_{\eta}
, we have

{f}_{N\oplus M}((N_{0},M_{0}),\sigma_{N\oplus M})={f}_{L^{\prime}}((N_{0},M_{0}),\sigma_{L^{\prime}}).

Finally we can simplify the calculation of
\bigl(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 6.15
.

We have

\bigl(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}.

Proof.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\bigl(g_{\rm skew}*_{\eta}\tilde{{f}}_{\eta}\bigr)*_{\eta}\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}\!q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{N\oplus M}((0,N_{0}\oplus M_{0}),\sigma_{N\oplus M})}\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+l(N,M,N_{0},M_{0})+{f}_{L^{\prime}}((0,L^{\prime}_{0}),\sigma_{L^{\prime}})}\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}\cdot\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}\int_{(N_{0},M_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf f},{\bf e}}^{\eta}(FN,FM,{\bf d})}q^{l(N,M,N_{0},M_{0})+{f}_{L^{\prime}}((0,L^{\prime}_{0}),\sigma_{L^{\prime}})}\cdot X^{p(L^{\prime},{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}\cdot{f}_{L^{\prime}}*_{\sigma_{L^{\prime}}}X_{L^{\prime}}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}.

∎

Notice that in the last expression above, the
q
-power is independent of the specific choice of submodules, and depends only on
M
and
N
.

Now we analyze

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}\!q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})},

where the middle term
L
in the triangle
N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N
is not located in the fundamental domain. In this case, there is an explicit construction of
FL
given as follows.
Let
U=\operatorname{Im}F\epsilon
, then there exists a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow V\rightarrow FM\xrightarrow{F\epsilon}U\rightarrow 0.

Then we have an exact sequence

0\rightarrow U\rightarrow F\Sigma N\rightarrow\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I\rightarrow 0.

Let
W=W^{\prime}\oplus P
, there exists a sequence

FN\xrightarrow{Fi}W\oplus V\xrightarrow{Fp}FM\xrightarrow{F\epsilon}F\Sigma N.

Finally,
FL=W\oplus V
.

Applying
F
to the triangle
N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N
leads to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN/N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL/L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/M_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Coker}Fp}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ker}Fi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\,0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}

Since
FL=W\oplus V
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FL/L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W/K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\oplus V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Considering
J
as a submodule of
V
, we have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\,0}

On the other hand, given submodule
K
of
W
, we can obtain an injection

\tau K\rightarrow\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I

by applying
\tau
and using the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau(W/K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,0,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where
\tau N_{0}
is given by the pullback which is unique up to isomorphism.

Lemma 6.16
.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau(W/K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,0,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\,0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F\epsilon}}

where
\overline{F\epsilon}
is induced by
{F\epsilon}
.

Proof.

By definition,
U=\operatorname{Im}F\epsilon
, therefore the composition of morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}

is exactly
F\epsilon
.
Thus we denote the composition of morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau N_{0}}

by
\overline{F\epsilon}
where
\overline{F\epsilon}(\bar{m})=F\epsilon(m)
.

Since
J\subseteq V=\operatorname{Ker}F\epsilon
,
F\epsilon(J)=0
and
\overline{F\epsilon}(FM/J)=F\epsilon(FM)=U\subseteq\tau N_{0}
. Therefore,
\overline{F\epsilon}
is well defined and the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM/J}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau N_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{F\epsilon}}

commutes. Moreover,
\operatorname{Ker}\overline{F\epsilon}=\operatorname{Ker}F\epsilon/J=V/J
and
\operatorname{Im}\overline{F\epsilon}=\operatorname{Im}F\epsilon=U
.

Thus the third row in the above four-row diagram is a long exact sequence.
∎

Let
{\bf d}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,L_{0}
,
{\bf n}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,N
,
{\bf w}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,W
,
{\bf k}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,K
and
{\bf j}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,J
.
We can construct a correspondence

\displaystyle\phi\colon\ \coprod_{\bf d}\coprod_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(W,V,{\bf d})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\coprod_{\bf d}\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d}),

\displaystyle(K,J)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(J,N_{0}),

where
J=M_{0}
and
N_{0}
is determined by
\tau N_{0}
