in the second row of the above two-row diagram.

Lemma 6.17
.

The map
\phi
is bijective.

Proof.

First we prove
\phi
is surjective. Given a long exact sequence in
\widetilde{A}

0\longrightarrow V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\longrightarrow}}FM\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F\epsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}F\Sigma N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{1}\\
\pi_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I\longrightarrow 0.

It gives a triangle in
\mathcal{C}

N\longrightarrow W\oplus I[-1]\oplus V\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow\tau N.

Applying
F
to the above triangle, we obtain the exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\oplus V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0,i\end{smallmatrix}\right)}

Given any
(J,N_{0})\in\coprod_{\bf d}\coprod_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}{\rm Gr}^{\epsilon}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}(FM,FN,{\bf d})
, by definition (see Section
4.4
), there exists

(W_{0},V_{0})\in\coprod_{\bf d}\coprod_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(W,V,{\bf d})
such that
the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\oplus V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM\,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{0}\oplus V_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0,i\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{N}}

Hence,
W_{0}=\pi_{1}(N_{0})
,
V=J
and then
\phi(\pi_{1}(N_{0}),J)=(J,N_{0})
.

We now prove
\phi
is injective. By the definition of
\phi
,
N_{0}=\pi_{1}^{-1}(K)
and
K=\pi_{1}(N_{0})
. Therefore, there is a unique
(K,J)
mapped to the given

(J,N_{0})
.
∎

Lemma 6.18
.

We have that
\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,\phi(K,J):=(\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,J,\underline{\operatorname{dim}}\,N_{0})=({\bf j},{\bf n}-({\bf w}-{\bf k}))
.

Proof.

Given
K\subseteq W
, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\Sigma N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau W^{\prime}\oplus I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau N_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\,0.}

Since both rows are short exact sequences, we can compute dimension vectors after applying
\tau^{-1}
as

\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}N_{0}=\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}FN+\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}K-\underline{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}W={\bf n}-({\bf w}-{\bf k}).

To complete the final calculation, we need an identity given in
[
18
]
.

Lemma 6.19

(
[
18
]
)
.

With the notation above, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\bf n}-({\bf w}-{\bf k}),{\bf m}-{\bf j}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf e}+{\bf f},{\bf m}+{\bf n}-{\bf e}-{\bf f}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf j}+{\bf k},{\bf w}+{\bf v}-{\bf j}-{\bf k}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}.

Remark 6.20
.

Recalling the definitions of
{f}_{M\oplus N}
and
{f}_{L}
, we can rewrite the above identity as

\displaystyle\langle{\bf n}-({\bf w}-{\bf k}),{\bf m}-{\bf j}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})=\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle+{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})-\frac{1}{2}.

Finally, we can simplify the calculation of
\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}
.

Lemma 6.21
.

We have
\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L}.

Proof.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)+\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot\int_{{\bf d}}\int_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}\int_{(M_{0},N_{0})\in{\rm Gr}_{{\bf e},{\bf f}}^{\epsilon}(FM,FN,{\bf d})}q^{\langle{\bf f},{\bf m}-{\bf e}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\cdot\int_{\bf d}\int_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\int_{(K,J)\in{{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(K,J,{\bf d})}}q^{\langle{\bf n}-({\bf w}-{\bf k}),{\bf m}-{\bf j}\rangle+{f}_{M\oplus N}((0,M_{0}\oplus N_{0}),\sigma_{M\oplus N})}\cdot X^{p(L,g)}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\int_{\bf d}\int_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\int_{(K,J)\in{{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(K,J,{\bf d})}}q^{\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle+{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}.

Now we focus on the exponent
\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle
of
q
in the above integration. Since
M
and
N
are rigid, so is
W\oplus V\oplus\Sigma^{-1}I
. Notice that

0\rightarrow V\rightarrow W\oplus V\rightarrow W\rightarrow 0

is a split short exact sequence. Therefore in this case, we also have

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(K,V/J)=0

for any
(K,J)\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf k}W\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf j}V
and hence

\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(K,V/J).

On the other hand, we can consider the mapping

\coprod_{\bf d}{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}FL\longrightarrow\coprod_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}{\rm Gr}_{\bf k}W\times{\rm Gr}_{\bf j}V

with affine fibers, induced by this split exact sequence, and observe that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(K,V/J)
is exactly the dimension of the fiber at
(K,J)
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\tilde{{f}}_{\epsilon}*_{\epsilon}\tilde{X}_{L}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\int_{\bf d}\int_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\int_{(K,J)\in{{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(K,J,{\bf d})}}q^{\langle{\bf k},{\bf v}-{\bf j}\rangle+{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\int_{\bf d}\int_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}\int_{(K,J)\in{{\rm Gr}^{\mathbf{0}_{WV}}_{{\bf k},{\bf j}}(K,J,{\bf d})}}q^{\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(K,V/J)+{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\int_{\bf d}\int_{L_{0}\in{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}FL}q^{{f}_{L}((0,L_{0}),\sigma_{L})}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\int_{\bf d}|{\rm Gr}_{\bf d}FL|\cdot q^{-\frac{1}{2}\langle{\bf d},{\bf l}-{\bf d}\rangle}\cdot X^{p(L,{\bf d})}

\displaystyle\quad=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L}.

∎

By Theorem
6.12
, Lemmas
6.15
and
6.21
, we have

Theorem 6.22

(
[
18
, Proposition 5.4.1]
)
.

In the cluster category
\mathcal{C}=D^{b}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}})/{\tau^{-1}\Sigma}
of a hereditary algebra
\widetilde{A}
, given two indecomposable coefficient-free rigid
objects
M,N\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
with

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(M,\Sigma N)=1

and two non-split triangles

N\rightarrow L\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{\epsilon}\Sigma N\qquad\text{and}\qquad M\rightarrow L^{\prime}\rightarrow N\xrightarrow{\eta}\Sigma M,

where
L^{\prime}
is located in the fundamental domain, then we have

\tilde{X}_{M}\cdot\tilde{X}_{N}=q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L}+q^{\frac{1}{2}\lambda(\operatorname{ind}M,\operatorname{ind}N)}\cdot\tilde{X}_{L^{\prime}}.
